MINISTRY OF PERSONNEL, PUBLIC GRIEVANCES 
AND PENSIONS 


(Department of Pension and Pensioners’ Welfare) 

Now Delhi, the 12th March, I98S 

S.O. 1246.—^Tn exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 300 read with clause (5) of article 148 
of the Constitution and after consultation with the Comp¬ 
troller and Auditor-General in relation to persons serving 
in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, the Pre.si- 
dent hereby makes the following nile.s further to amend 
the Central Civil Services (Pension) Rules, 1972, namely: — 

1. (1) These Rules may be called the Central Civil 

Services (Pension) (Amendment) Rules, 1986. 

(2) They shall come into force from the date of 
publication In the Official Gazette 

2. In the Central Civil Services (Pension) Rules, 1972, in 
rule 6fl— 


(a) for sub-rule (2), the following sub-rule shall be 
substituted, namely:— 

"(2) Every case of delayed payment of gratuity shall 
be considered by the Secretary of the Adminiitra- 
tive Ministry or the DetJortment in respect or its 
employees and the employees of its attached and 
subordinate offices and where the #^cretary of the 
Ministry or the Denartment is satisfled that the 
delay in the payment of gratuity was caused on 
account of administrative lapse, the Secretary of 
the Ministry or the Depaitment shall sanction pay¬ 
ment of interest." 

(b) for sub-rule (3) the following sub-rule shall be 

substituted, namely:— 

"(3) The Administrative Ministry or the Department 
shall issue Piesidential sanction for the nayment of 
interest after the Secretary has sanctioned the 
payment of interest under sub-rule (2).’’ 

(c) for sub-rule (4) the follotving sub-rule shall be 

substituted, namely;— 

"(4) Tn all cases whore the payment of interest has 
been sanctioned bv the Serretary of the Adminls- 
frath'e Ministry or the Denartment, such M’inlstiv 
or the Denartment shall fix the rcsnunsihilitv and 
take disciplinary action against the Government 
servant or servants who are found resnon.sible for 
the delay in the payment of grafu/ty.” 

[No, 7(I3)|85-P&PW] 
HAZARA SINGH, Dy. Secy.

December, 1981) of the rules was printed in 1982.

LICENCE CANCELLATION ORDER 
S.O. 1256.—M/s. Solid State Devices India Limited, 
Patnneheru, Hyderabad were granted an import licence bear¬ 
ing No. P|D|2249897|C|XX|96|Wi35 dated 30-9-85 for a 
C.I.F, Value of Rs. 2,46,(K)0 for import of Restricted 
Spares required for operation & Maintenance of the Capital 
Goods installed or used by the licence holder including 
spares of ancillary equipment Control & Laboratory equip¬ 
ment & Safety appliances. The party has applied for grant 
of Duplicate Cusioms & Exchange Control Purpose copies 
of the aforesaid Import Licence on the ground that the 
original Customs & Exchange Control purpose copies have 
been lost. The total amount for which the duplicutc copies 
of the licence is required for the full value of the licence 
(i.e.) Rs. 2,46,000 (Rupees two lakhs forty six thousand 
only). 

In support of their contention, the applicant h.as filed an 
affidavit on stamped paper duly attested by a Public notary. 
The applicant has also under taken to return the licensing 
authority concerned the original customs & Exchange con¬ 
trol purpose copies of the licence, if the same are trac^ 
or found later on. 

I am satisfied that the original Customs purpose and 
Exchange Control copy of licence No, P|D|2249897|C|XX|- 
96iWl85 dated 30-9-85 have been lost and that duplicate 
Customs & Exchange Control purpo.se copy of licence should 
be issued to the applicant. The original Customs & Ex¬ 
change Control copy of licence No, P|D|2249897|C|XX|96|- 
W|85 dated 30-9-85 are hereby cancelled,

New Delhi, the 10th February, 1986 
CANCELLADON ORDER 

S.O. 1257—M/s Tntercralt Sales Corpn A-I/46 S'afder- 
jung Enclave, New Delhi were granted advance licence No. 
P|K|04164691C|XX|96|D|85 dated 11-9-83 for import of 
1000 kes. of Un-manufactured Ivory for CIF. value of 
Rs. 8,30,000. 

The firm have reported that Exchange Control copy the 
said licence has been misplaced after having been registered 
with Asslt, CoUoctor of Customs, CWC Gurgaon Road, 
New Delhi and remitted partly upto Rs. 6.02,952-90 leaving 
unreraitted unutilised balance value of Rs. 247047. 

The firm have filed an affidavit in support of the above 
statement as required under paras 85 to 88 of Hand Book 
if Import Export Procedures 1985-88 I am satisfied the 
original Exchange Control copy of the said advance licence 
has been misplaced. 

In exercise of the powers conferred on me under section 
9(5) of Imvort (Control) order, 1955 dated 7-12-1955 as 
amended upto-date, I hereby order cancellation of the said 
original Exchange Control copy of the advance licence. 

The applicant’s case will now bo considered for issue of 
duplicate Exchange Control purpose copy of Import licence 
No. PIK|0416469|C|XX|96|D|85 dated 11-9-1988 for un- 
remitted value of Rs. 2,47,047-10 in accordance with para 
85 to 88 of Hand Book Import Export Procedure, 1985-88. 

[No. Adv|Lic|UDES|731AM.86lALS.I|CLA12030]

S.O. 1259.—^In exercise of the powers conferred by sec¬ 
tion 3 of the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 (10 of 1955) 
the Central Government hereby makes the following Order 
further to amend Textile (Production by Powerlooms) Cont¬ 
rol Order, 1956, namely ;— 

1. This Order may be called the Textiles (Production 

by Powerlooms) Control Amendment Order, 1966. 

2. In sub-clause (1) of clause 6 of the Textile (Pro¬ 

duction by Powerlooms) Control Order, 1956, in 
the first proviso, for the expression, “Rs. 600 
(rupees six hundred only)", the expression "Rs, 2.50 
(rupees two hundred and fifty only)” shall bo subs¬ 
tituted.

New Delh, the 13th March, 1986 

S.O. 1260.—Whereas by notification of the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum S.O, 1824 dated 26-5-84 
under .sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroreum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government declared 
its Intention to acquire the right of . user in the lands speci¬ 
fied in the Schedule appended to that notification for the 
purpose of laying pipeline; 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under sub¬ 
section Cl) Of Section 6 of the said Act, submattcd report 
to the Government; 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the Schedule appened to this 
notification; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (D of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user In tbe 
said lands specified in ihc Schedule appended to this notifi¬ 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exorcise oi power conferred by sub-section 
(4) of that section. Central Government directs that the 
right Of user in the said lands shaU instead of vesting in 
Central Government vests on this date of the publication of

S.O. 1265.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the publig interest that lor the 
transport of petroleum from NKAK to NK GGSI in Guja¬ 
rat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural Gas 
Commission. 


[Part II— Sec. 3(ii) ] 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary td acqiime the right of user 
in the I'and described la the Schedule annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of *he Section 3 of the petroleum and Mine¬ 
rals Pipelinci. (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (30 of 1962), the Central Goveiument hereby 
declares it» intention, to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said laud may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object 
to the laying of the pipeline under the land to the Com¬ 
petent Authority, Oif and Natural Gas Commission, Con¬ 
struction and Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, 
Vadodara, (390 009). 

And every person making uich an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by legal practitioner.

S.O. 1274.—Whereas it appears to the Central Ooscin- 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from Dikhow Junction Paint to 
l.akwa C.T.P, in Sibsagar Dist., Assam. Pipeline should be 
laid by the Oil and Natural Gas Commission ; 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
stijb pipelines, it is necessary to acquire the Right of User 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; , 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of the section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Mineral Pipelines (Acquisition of Right User in land) Act, 
1%2 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declared 
its iritentibn to acquire the right of user therein; 

Provided that any person interested in the said land may, 
withiii 21 days from the date of this notification ol^'ect to 
the laying of the pipelines under the land to the Competent 
Authority viz. the Deputy Commissioner, Sibsagar, Assam ; 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person 
or by a legal practitioner.

S.O. 1279.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the transport 
of Petroleum from G44 to G-52 to Gcleki GGS-3 In Sibsagar 
Distt., Assam, Pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural 
Gas Commission. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline, it is necessary to acquire the right of user in 
the land described in the schedule annexed hereto; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (I) of the Section 3 of the Petroleum and 
Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the 
I.nnd) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962). the Central Gorernment 
hereby declares its intention to acqtiirc the right of user 
therein; 

Provided that any person intere.sted in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification, object to 
the laying of the pipelines under the land to the Competent 
Authority, viz. the Deputy Commissioner, Sibsagar, Assam. 

And every person making such an objection .shall also 
.state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or 
by legal practitioner.

S.O. 1282.—Whereas by notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Petroleum S.O. 1373 dated 
7-4-84 under sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum 
and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in 
Land) Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government 
declared its intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands specified in the schedule appended to that notification 
for the purpose f laying pipeline. 

And whereas the Competent Authority has under Sub- 
Section (1) of Section 6 of the said Act, suWitted report 
to the Government: 

And further whereas the Central Government has, after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 

l^Otifi^'ntinn ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
•sub-scctiop.. (1) of the Section 6 of the said Act, the Cen- 
trai Government hereby declares that the right of user in 
the said lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
■notification hereby acquired for laying the pipeline; 

And further in exorcise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (4) of that section, the Central Government directs 
that the right of iTser in the said lands shaU instead of 
vesting in Central Government, vests on this date of the 
publication of this declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas 
Commission free from encumbrances.

{DEPARTMENT OF SPACE) 

Bangalore, March 17, 1986 

S.O. 1309.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the pro¬ 
viso to article 309 of the Constitution, the President hereby 
makes the following rules further to amend the Department 
of Space Employees’ (Classification, Control and Appeal) 
Rules, 1976, namely:— 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Department of Space 

Employees' (Classification, Control and Appeal) Am¬ 
endment Rules, 1986. 

(2) They shall come Into force on the date of their pub¬ 
lication In the Official Gazette. 

2. In the Schedule to the Department of Space Employees’ 
(Oassification, Control & Appeal) Rules, 1976, under the 
heading "Auxiliary Propulsion Systems Unit (AP3U)’’,

MINISTRY OF LABOUR 
New Delhi, the 10th March, 1986 
S.O. 1315.^—In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 
S of the Beedi Workers Welfare Fund Act, 1976 (62 of 1976) 
read with sub-rule 721 of Rule 3 of the Beedi Wo kers 
Wclfaic Find Rules, 1978, the Central Government hereby 
makes the following amendment in the Notification No. S.O, 
3039 dated 3rd/6th September, 1984 published at pages 
2845-46 of part II Section 3 sub-section (ii) of the Gazette 
of India dated 22nd September, 1984. 

In the said Notification, against serial number 10 and in 
para 2, the following shall be substituted namely :— 

10. Welfare Administrator Ahmedabad, Gujarat. 

2. Under rule 16 of the said rules, the Central Govern¬ 
ment hereby fixes Ahmedabad to be the headquarters of the 
said Committee.

S.O. I318.—Wherea» Messrs Indian Express (Madurai) 
Private l-iinited, 137-Kainarajar Road, Madurai (Tamil Nadu) 
(TN/84f;fi) (hereinafter referred to as the said establishment) 
have applied for exemption under sub-section (2B) of Section 
17 of the Employe'es' Provident Funds and Miscellaneous 
Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to 
as the said Act) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
regular employees of the said establishment are, without 
making any separate contribution or payment of premium, in 
enjoymdit of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of 
the Life Insurance Corporation of India in the nature of Life 
Insurance which are more favourable to such employees than 
the benefits admissible under the Employees’ Deposit Linked 
Insurance Scheme, 1976 (hereinafter referred to as the said 
Scheme) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sirb-sectlon (2B) of section 17 of the said Act and subject to 
the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed hereto, the 
Central Government hereby exempts the regular employees 
of the said establishment from the operation of all the provi- 
sions of the said Scheme for a period of three yean. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment 
shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner. Tamil Nadu maintain such accounts and P'‘o- 
vide such facilities for insnectlon, as the Central Govcrnmw'nt 
may direct from time to time, 

2. The employer shall pay such inspection charges as the

clause fa) of suh-aection (3A) of section 17 of the said Act. 
within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the adminIsfraEon of tlie Group 
Insurance Scheme, jnclnding maintenance of accounts, subm's- 
sion of rcfvrn.s, payment of insurance premia, transfer of 
accounts, payment of inspection charge.s etc. shall be borne 
by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishment, a coov of the rules of the Qroiro Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, alongwith a translat'on of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majority of the employees. 

5. Whereas an employee, who is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
eslablishment exempted under the said Act, is employed in 
his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrpj him 
as a member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay nc~es- 
sary premium in respect of him to the I.ife Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6. The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance Scheme 
appropriate!}', if the benefits available to the employees under 
the said Scheme ar:^ enhanced, so that the benefits available 
under the Grotm Insurance Scheme are more favourable to 
the emplo}'ecs than the benefits admissible under the said 
Scheme. 

7. NotV'ithslanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this Scheme bo less than the amount that 


would be payable had employee been covered under the said 
Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the legal 
heir/nominee of the employee as compensation. 

8. Nc amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Region.pl Provident Fund Commissioner, Tamil Nadu and 
where any amendment is likely to afferl adversely the interest 
of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner 
shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable opportunity 
to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Where, for any reason, the employees of the said esta- 
bh'shment do not remain covered under the Group Ipsprance 
Scheme of the Lffc Insurance Corporation of (ndia as plre.'dy 
adopted by the sa/d establishment, or the benefits to the 
employees under this Scheme are reduced in any manner, the 
exemption shall he liable to be cancelled, 

10. Where, for any reason, the emjiloyer fails to pa” 
the premium etc, within the due date, as fixed by t){o I.ife 
Insurance Corporation of India, and the Policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. Tn case of def.anlt^ if any made by the employer In 
payment of premium the responsibility for navmcnt of assu¬ 
rance benefits to the nominees or the leua) heirs of deceased 
members who wotrld have been covered under the said Scheme

p foyer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corporation of India shall en.siirB

hems of the dece.ascd member entitled,for it and in any case 
wiih-n One month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

SCHEDULE

nPBfPPrinlcly» if the benefits available to the employees under 
the said Scheme arc enhanced, so that the benefits available 
under the Group Insurance Scheme are more favourable to 
the employees than the benefits admissible under the said 
Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this Scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the said 
Scheme, ihc employer shall pay the difference to the legal 
heir/nominee of tl* employee as compensation. 

8. No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Pune and 
where any amendment is likely to affect adversely the interest 
of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner 
shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable opportunity 
to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Wlhere, for any reason, the employees of the said esta¬ 
blishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as already 
adopted by the said eslablishment, or the benefits to the 
employees under this Scheme are reduced in atvy manner, the 
exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to pay 
the premium etc. within the due date, as fixed by the Life 
Insuiance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any, made by the employer in 
payment of premium the responsibility for payment of assu¬ 
rance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of deceased 
members who would have been covered under the said Scheme 
but for grant of this exemption, shall be that of the em¬ 
ployer.

[Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


S.O. 1321.—Whereas Messrs Ranbaxy Laboratories Limited 
Okhla, New Delhi-110020 (DL/682) (hereinafter referred to 
as the said establishment) have applied for exemption under 
sub-section (2A) of section 17 of the Hraployec.'i' Provident 
Lunds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) 
(heieinafter referred to as thq said Act) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are. without making any 
separate contribution or payment of piemium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of the 
India in the nature of Life Insuiance which arc more favour¬ 
able to such employees than the benefits admissible under 
the Employees’ Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 
(hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of section 17 of the said Act and in conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Government of India in 
the Ministry of Labour, S.O. 3344 dated the 30-8-1982 and 
subject to the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed 
heicto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme for a further period of three years with effect 
front 18-9-1985 upto and inclusive of the 17-9-1988. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment 
shall submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner, Delhi and mainlain such accounts and pro¬ 
vide such facilities for inspection, as the Central Government 
may direct from time to tunc. 

2. The employer shall pay-, such inspection charges as the 
Central Government may. from time to time, direct under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3A) of section 17 of the said Act, 
within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the Group 
Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, subm s- 
.sion of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer of 
accounts, payment of inspection tharges etc. shall be borne 
by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as and 
when amended, along with a translation of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majoiity of the employees. 

■ 5, Whereas an employee, who is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment exempted iind^^he said Act, is employed in 
his establi.'.hraent, Ihe cmplo^W shall immediately enrol him 
as a member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay neces¬ 
sary premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6. The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance Scheme 
appropriately, if the benefits available to the employees under 
the said Scheme are enhariced, so that the benefits available 
under the Group Insurance Scheme b’’o more favourable to 
the employees than the benefits admissible under the said 
Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this Scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the said 
Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the legal 
helr/nomince of the employee as compensation; 

8. No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Delhi and 
where any amendment Is likely to effect adversely the interest 
of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner 
shall before giving his approval, ^ive a reasonable opportunity 
to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Where, for any reason, the employees of the said esta¬ 
blishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as already 


adopted by the said establishment, or the benefits to the 
employees under this Scheme aie reduced in any manner, the 
exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason ,the employer fails to pay 
the premium etc. williin the due date as fixed by the Life 
Insuiance Corporation of India, and‘the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payment of premium the responsibility for payment of assu¬ 
rance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of deceased 
members who would have been covered under ,thc said Scheme 
but for grant ot mis exemption, shall be that of the em¬ 
ployer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum assured to the nomineeAegal 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any case 
within one month from the receipt of claim complete in 
all respects.

S.O. 1323.—Wliereas Messrs Jila Sahakari Kcndriya Bank 
Maryadih, Sadar Ba?ar, Morcna, Madhya Pradesh (M.P./ 
1091) (hereinafler referred to as the said establishment) 
have applied for exemption under sub-section (2A) of section 
17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds and Miscellaneous 
Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (hereinafter referred to as 
the said Act) ; 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution or payment of premium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of 
India in the nature of Life Insurance which are more favour¬ 
able to such eranloyoes than the benefits admissible under 
the Emnloyees’ Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme, 1976 
fheremafter referred to as the said Scheme) ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2A) of section 17 of the said Act and in conti¬ 
nuation of the notification of the Gov:cmmcnt of India in 
the Ministry of Labour, S.O. 3950 dated the 8-11-1982 and 
subject to the conditions specified in the Schedule annexed 
hereto the Central Government hereby exempt.! the sa'd 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme for a further period of three years with effect 
from 27-11-1985 upto and inclusive of the 26-11-1988. 

1691 GIl85~8 


SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund Commis¬ 
sioner Madhya Pradesh and maintain such accounts and 
provide such facilities for inspection as the Central Govern¬ 
ment may direct from time to time. 

2. The employer shall pay such .Inspection charges as the 
Central Government may, from time to time, diiect i.nder 
clause (a) of sub-section 13A) of section 17 of the said Act 
within 15 days from the close of every month, 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the Group 
Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts, submis¬ 
sion of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer of 
accounts, payment of inspection charges etc, shall be homo 
by the employer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board of the 
establishments, a copy of the rules of the Group Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Governmont and, us and 
when amended, alongwith a translation of the salient features 
thereof, in the language of the majority of the employees. 

5. Whereas an employee, who is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident Fund or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment exempted under the said Act. is employed in 
his establishment, the employer shall immediately enrol him 
as a member of the Group Insurance Scheme and pay neces¬ 
sary premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Grotip, Insurance Scheme 
appropriately, if the benefits availably to the employees under 
the said Scheme are enhanced so that the benefits available 
under the Group Insurance Scheme are more favourable to 
the employees than the benefits admissible under the said 
Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group Insu- 
surance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this scheme be less than the amount that 
would be payable had employee been covered under (he said 
Scheme, the employer shall pay the differejice to the legal 
heir/nominee of the employee as compensation. 

8 . No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insurance 
Scheme; shall be made without the prior approval of the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner, Madhva Pradesh 
and where any amendment is likely lo effect adversely the 
interest of the employees, the Regional Provident Fund Com- 
mlss’ouer shall before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their point of view. 

9. Wliere, for any reason, the employees of the said esta¬ 
blishment do not remain covered under the Group Insurance 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as already 
adonted by the said establishment, or the benefits to the 
employees under this Scheme are reduced In any manner, the 
exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Wltere, for any reason, the employer fails to pay the 
premium etc. within the due date, as fixed by the L’fe Insu¬ 
rance Corporation of India, and the policy Is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
payment of premium the resnonsibilitv for payment of assu¬ 
rance benefits to the nominees or the legal bei^s of deceased 
members who would have been covered under the said 5chcme 
but for grant of this exemption, shall,, bo that of the em¬ 
ployer, 

12. Unon the death of the members covered uti^er the 
Scheme the l ife Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum, assured to .the nominee or the 
legal heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and In 
anv case within one month from the receipt of claim complete 
in all respects.

S.O. 1325.—'Whi.Teas Messrs, M.P. State Tourism Deve¬ 
lopment CorporiUion Limited 5th floor Gangotari Complex, 
T.T. Nagar, Bhopal (.M P./3S02), (hereinafter referred to as 
the said establishment) Lave applied for exemption i nder sub¬ 
section (2A) of Section 17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds 
& Mis-cllaneous Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) (herein¬ 
after referred to as the said Act); 

And whereas, the Central Government is satisfied that the 
employees of the said establishment are, without making any 
separate contribution or payment of premium, in enjoyment 
of benefits under the Group Insurance Scheme of the Life 
Insurance Corporation of India in the nature of Life Insu¬ 
rance which are more favourable to such employees than the 
benefits admissible under the Employees’ Deposit Linked In- 
surance Scheme, 1976 (hereinafter referred to as the said 
Scheme); 

Now therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub¬ 
section (2A) of section 17 of the said Act and subject to 
the conditions specified in the schedule annexed hereto, the 
Central Government hereby exempts the said establishment 
from the operation of all the provisions of the said Scheme 
for a period of three years. 

SCHEDULE 

1. The employer in relation to the said establishment shall 
submit such returns to the Regional Provident Fund Com¬ 
missioner, Madhya iPiadesh Maintain such accounts and pro¬ 
vide such facilities for Inspection, as the Central Government 
may direct from time to time. 


2. The employer sliaJl pay such inspection charges as the 
Central Government may, from tinjo to time, direct under 
clause (a) of sub-section (3A) of Section 17 of the said Act, 
within 15 days from the close of every month. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of the Group 
Insurance Scheme, including maintenance of accounts sub¬ 
mission of returns, payment of insurance premia, transfer of 
accounts, payment of inspection charges etc, shall be borne 
by the employer. 

4. The employer shall de.splay on the Notice Board of 
the esablishment, a copy of the rules of the Gioup Insurance 
Scheme as approved by the Central Government and, as 
and when amended, alongwith a translation of the stdient 
features thereof, in the language of the majority of the em¬ 
ployees. 

5. Whereas an employee, who is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident t^und or the Provident Fund of an 
establishment exempted under the said Act, is employed in 
his establishment, the employer thall immediately enrol him 
as a member of the Croup Insurance Scheme and pay ne es- 
sary premium in respect of him to the Life Insurance Cor¬ 
poration of India. 

6 . The employer shall arrange to enhance the benefits 
available to the employees under the Group Insurance Scheme 
appropriately, if the beneflt.s available to the employees under 
the said Scheme are enhanced, so that the benefits available 
under the Group Insurance Scheme are more favourable to 
the employees than the benefits admissible under the said 
Scheme. 

7. Notwithstanding anything contained in the Group In¬ 
surance Scheme, if on the death of an employee the amount 
payable under this Scheme be less than the amount' that 
would be payable had employee been covered under the said 
Scheme, the employer shall pay the difference to the legal 
heir/nominee of the employee as compensation. 

8 . No amendment of the provisions of the Group Insu¬ 
rance Scheme, shall be made without the prior approval of 
the Reoional Provident Fund Commissioner. Madhya Pradesh 
and where any amendment is likely to effect adversely fhe 
interest of the emph'vees, the Regional Provident Fund 
Commissioner shall before giving his approval, give a reaso-’- 
able opportunity to the employees to explain their point of 
view. 

9. 'Where, for any raasoii. the employee of the said esiRb- 
lishment do not remain covered under the Group Insuran e 
Scheme of the Life Insurance Corporation of India as al¬ 
ready adopted by the said establishment, or the benefits to 
the employees under this Scheme are reduced in any manner, 
the exemption shall be liable to be cancelled. 

10. Where, for any reason, the employer fails to pay the 
premium etc, within tlie due date, as fixed by the Life In¬ 
surance Corporation of India, and the policy is allowed to 
lapse, the exemption is liable to be cancelled. 

11. In case of default, if any made by the employer in 
pa 3 nnent of premium the responsibility for payment of assu¬ 
rance benefits to the nominees or the legal heirs of deceased 
members who would Iiave been covered under the said Scheme 
but for grant of this exemption, shall be that of the employer. 

12. Upon the death of the members covered under the 
Scheme the Life Insurance Corporation of India shall ensure 
prompt payment of the sum Bwut ed to the nominee/legal 
heirs of the deceased member entitled for it and in any case 
within one month from the receipt of claim complete in all 
respects.

New Delhi, the 12th March, 1^86 

S.O, 1326,—In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Central Government In¬ 
dustrial Tribunal No. 2, Dhanbad as shown in the Annexure, 
in the industrial dispute between the employers in relation 
to the management of Chapapur—I Colliery of Messrs tas- 
tern Coalfields Limited and their workman, which was re¬ 
ceived by the Central Government on the 10th March, 1986. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
tribunal (NO. 2) AT DHANBAD 

PRESENT : 

Shri I. N. Sinha, Presiding Officer. 

Reference No. 27 of 1985 

In the matter of Industrial Disputes under Section 10(1)- 
(d) of the LD. Act., 1947. 

PARTIES : 

Employers in relation to the management of Chapapur-1 
Colliery of Messrs. Eastern Coalfields Limited and 
their workmen. 

APPEARANCES: 

On behalf of the employers—Shri R. S. Murthy, 

Advocate. 

On behalf of the workmen—Shri S. Bose, Secretary, 
RCMS Union. 

STATE : Bihar INDUSTRY : Coal 

Dated, the 27th February, 1986 
AWARD 

The Govt, of India, Ministry of Labour in exercise of 
the powers conferred on them under Section 10(l)(d) of the 
I.D. Act,, 1947 has referred the following dispute to this 
Tribunal for adjudication under Order No. L-20012(357)/84- 
D.IIKA), dated, the 19th March, 1985. 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management of Chapapur-I 
Colliery of Messrs Eastern Coalfields Limited, Nirsha 
Area in not allowing Shri Trinath Sethi. O.B.R. 
to resume duty is ju.stifled ? If not, to what relief 
is the workman entitled 7” 

In this case both the parties filed their respective W.S. 
Thereafter several adjournments were granted to the parties 
for filing settlement. Ultimately on 21-1-86 Shri R. S. Murthy, 
Advocate for the employers appeared before me and filed 
a memorandum of settlement. 1 find that the terms of settle¬ 
ment are fair and proper and beneficial to both the parties. 
Accordingly I accept the same and pass an award in terms 
of the memorandum of settlement which forms part of the 
Award as AnnexUre. 

Dt : 27-2-86. 

1. N. SINHA, Presiding Officer 
INo. L-20012/357/84-D.UI-(A)] 

ANNEXURE 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL NO. 2 DHANBAD 
In the Matter of Reference No. 27 of 1985 
PARTIES : 

Employers In relation to the Management of Chapapur-I 
Colliery of Eastern Coalfields Ltd., PO : Mugma, 
Dt : Dhanbad. 


AND 

Their Workmen 

Joint Petition of Employers and Workmen for Com¬ 
promise 

The abovementioned employers and workmen most res¬ 
pectfully beg to submit this joint petition. 

(1) That the management and the worknten have mu¬ 

tually and jointly negotiated the matter with a view 
to arriving at an aii.ivuble and overall settlement. 

( 2 ) That as a Jesuit of such negotiations, die Manage¬ 
ment and the woikmen have mutually agreed to 
settle the matter covered by the aforesaid reference 
on the following terms and conditions :— 

(a) It is agreed that the identity of Sri Fritiath Sethi, 
the workman concerned, will be jointly estab¬ 
lished and varified by the Manager of Chapapur-I 
Colliery and Si-j Siddheshwar Prasad Singh, Presi¬ 
dent Regional Committee. RCMS, Dhanbad: 

(b) It is agreed that after the identity and genuineness 
of Sri Trinalh Sethi is mutually estabJisJjcd ,as 
indicated in Clause (a) above, he will he provided 
employment by the Managorr.ent as O ver Bui den 
Remover in piece rated Group-Ill, subject to his 
being medically found fit by the medical offi er 
of the Management. Within a fortnight of this 
petition being accepted by this Hoii’ble Tribunal. 

(c) It is agreed that the workman concerned will not 

be paid any back wages or any other benefit 
for the past period and that he will be entitled 
to wages only from the date of his employment 
in terms of this agreement. He will, however, 
have the benefit of continuity of service. 

(d) It is agreed that this is art overall agreement/ 
settlement :n full and fin.il settlement of all the 
claims of the workman concerned and the sponsor¬ 
ing union arising out of the above t’-for«nce. 

(3) That the Mariagcment and the workmen consider 

that the aforesaid agreement and terms and cordi- 
tions are fair, just and reasonable for both the 
parties.

vvorkraan jointly pray that the Hon’blc Tribunal may be 
pleased to give an award in terms of this joint compromise 
petition and is pose of the reference accordingly

The Government of India, Ministry of Labour in exercise 
of the powers conferred on them under Section 10(1 )(d) of 
the I.D. Act., 1947 has referred the following dispute to this 
Tribunal for adjudication under Order No, L-20012(53)/82- 
D,III(A), dated, the 18th June, 1982. 

schedule 

“Whether the demand of the workmen of Damoda col¬ 
liery in Area No, 1 .if Messis Bharat Coking Coal 
Limited. Post Office Nawagarh District Dhanhad 
for giving Gradc-B to Shri Babulal Roy, Pay Loader 
Operator is justified 7 If so, to what relief is the 
workman concerned entitled 7” 

The case of the workmen is that the concerned workman 
Shri Babulal Roy is a permanent employee of Damoda col¬ 
liery of M/s. B.C.C. Ltd, He was Car driver till November, 
1976. The Personnel of Directorate of BCCL vide letter dated 
30-10/1-11-76 directed ihc concerned workman to report 
for training in operation services in development and con¬ 
struction division of BCCL at Vikash Bhawan Jharia. The 
concerned w'orkman reported there on the concerned work¬ 
man received training in operation and maintenance of Dum¬ 
per, Pay loader etc. at Vikash Bhawan Jharia, He continued 
to serve in the development and construction division till 
January, 1981 when he was released front the said division 
and posted in Area No, I of M/s. BCCL in the capacity of 
pay loader operator. He performed his duties as pay loader 
operator in Mudidih and other projects within area No, I 
and ultimately he was posted i.n Damoda Colliery as Pay 
loader operator and his pay was fixed in the Excavation 
Grade-D. While he was working as pay loader operator In 
the Execavation grade-D be learnt from his associates th:.f 
Excavation Grade-D r ite of wages was being paid on similar 
job in Other places. On being informed about the said dis¬ 
crimination made by Ibe management, the local union of 


tiic workmen made a representULion before the Area Oone-

The case of the mauagernont is that the pay s.rUL'ture o.. 
Coal Mmuig industry are governed by Coal Wage.^oard

under NcWA-il for lonnation of standardi.sation comriiitiee 
for fixation of proper guides to dificient categories of woikmen 
not covered earlier and JBCCl is the final authoiity to decide 
the norms for fixation of different grades to different cate¬ 
gories of workmen not covered earlier. The JBCCl has 
prescribed the norms on tbe basis of which pay loader opera¬ 
tors are to be fixed in different grades, namely, Grade-B, 
C, D, and E. The union which has sponsored the dispute 
being a party to the said agreement was bound to honour the 
recommendalion of the JBCCl made in respect of pay 
loader opcratois. According to the scheme of pay roader 
operator is initially put in Grade-E. After completion, 
of minimum of two years of experience and having* proved 
his ability to operate all types of pay loaders of capacities of 
2 C.M. or less and possessing sufficent skill and knowledge 
of running repairing and maintenun e of pay loader operator 
is promoted lO Grade-D. Thereafter he is promoted to 
Grade-C and then lo Grade-B on the basis of the punns 
laid down by the sefwme formulated by JBCCl. The con¬ 
cerned workman was originally a car driver, appointed on 
1-11-76. He was sent lo development and construction divi¬ 
sion to acquire training in operating pay loaders. He com¬ 
pleted his training and obtained heavy vehicle licence. The 
pay loaders were deployed at Damoda colliery in the year 
1979 and therefore the concerned workman was employed 
as pay loader operator. He was fixed in Gradc-D by letter 
dated 28/29-1-1981 in persuance of the recommendation of 
the DPC and since then hr has been rontinuing in Grade-D. 
He has lo complete 3 yeans of experience in operating aU 
types of pay loaders upto 4 cubic meter capacity and to prove 
his skill and knowledge in proper repairing and maintenance 
of pay loaders for being promoted to Gradc-C. As the con¬ 
cerned workman did not complete three years of experience, 
his case for promotion to Grade-C was not considered. The 
pay loader operator for promotion from Grade-C to Grade-B 
must be highly skilled in respect of repairing and maintenance 
and operation of pay loaders of any capacity aViove 4 C.M. 
and must haye proved his worth for 5 years maintaining good 
records of service. The demand of the hnion to put the 
concerned workman in Grade-B from the very beginning of 
his career as Pay loader operator is unreasonable and un¬ 
justified. The concerned v.'Oi'kman is not entitled to any relief. 

The question to be determined is whether the concerned 
workman is entitled for Orade-B pf Pay loader operator 
since 1981. 

The management and the workmen have each examined 
one witness in support of their respective cases. The workmen 
have produced documents which have been marked Ext. W-1 
to W-9 and the manag-tment has produced only one docu¬ 
ment which has been marked as Ext. M-1. 

It is the admitted case of the parties that the coucerPed 
workman is working as Pay loader operator in Damoda col¬ 
liery and is placed in excavation Orade-D. It is admitted bv 
the workmen in para 4 of their rejoinder to the W.S. filed 
by the management that the pay structure are governed by 
Ctoal Wage Board Recommendation followed by NCWA-I

and NCWA-U as well as by viriue of practice prevailing in

It will appear from Ext. W-1 that para-12 of deal with pay

ing all types of pay loaders of capacity of 4 C.M. and above.

ment aiui_ should be able to undertake niiuor rnnniiig lepair. 
He must hold valid heavy vehicle driving licence. He should

having 5 years ot e.\pcricijcc and capable of operating all 
types of pay loaders ol capacity of 4 C.M. and above. A 
pay loader operator'in order to get Grade-C should be skilled

types of pay loaders of capacity of less than 4 C.M. but

should be a skilled workman having 2 years experience in 
heavy Muipment and in heavy vehicle capable of operating 
all tyieS' of pay loaders of capacity of 2 C.M, and less. It 
will thus appear from the above that (1) the number of yeais 
of exper'cnce in driving a certain type of pay loader and 
(2) the size of pay ro.rder avo deciding factors in giving speci¬ 
fic grade to the pay loader operator. 

Ext. W-6 dated 30-10-7611-11-^6 is an office oidcr which

as pay loader onerntor from 2S|29-I-8l, For pro¬

all fvne of pav loaders of capneitv of 4 C, M. nnd above. 
It will thus appear that the case of the copcerred work¬

MW-1 SbrJ O. N. Choubev was working as Aeent of

given Grade-B. WW-1 is the concerned workman himself.

Rov and in the absence of the .said evidence it cannot be

wh'ch was boint; operated by the concerned workman. It

ind had not oneraicd a pay lo.nder of the canaeity which 
ceptd entitle h'm for promotion to either Orade-C or 
Grad--R at .the time when (he industrial dismue was rabed. 
The ch'm of the wo'-kmen therefore for pay loader operator 
Grade-B does not appear to be justified. The concerned 
workman however has now gained experience of more than 
3 vear? and deserves to be considered for promotion by the 
management to the higher grade and T hope that the manage¬ 
ment will consider his case for nromotion to the appropriate 
grade as he has gained experience of year*. 

In the result I hold that the demand of the workmen 
of-D.-imodri colliery of Mis. B.C.C. l td. (or, giving prnde-B 
to the concerned workman Babulal Roy as Pay loader 
Onerator is not justified and he is not entitled to any relief 
on the date Of reference.

Aren in relation to their RaWanwafa Colliery in retiring

By then' order No, L-11012(14)/84-D. 11(B) doled 28-S-85 
the following dispute has been referred for adjudication 
under Section 10(l)(d) of tlie Induslrial Disputes Act, 1947;- 

“Whether the action of the management of Aerodrome 
Officer of Civil Aviation Department of Dabolim 
Airport in terminating the services of Miss Maha- 
nanda Narvekar, Sweepress with effect from 25-2-82 
and not re-employing her even thouei! 'fieri were

2. The workman Miss Mahananda Narveknr was in the 
service of Civil Aviation Department of Dafiolim Airport

3. By the written statement In the first place it is alleged

4. On the above pleadings the folliwing issues arise for 
determination and my findings thereon are:—

REASONS 

5. The main question which poses for determination in 
this reference naturally would be whether the Civil Aviation 
Department '■in whose service :he workman was, is an indu¬ 
stry or not. As defined in Section 2fJ) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act where it means any business, trade under¬ 
taking, manufacture or calling of employers and include 
any <^mg, service, employment, handicraft or industrial 
occupation or a vocation of workmen, 

6, The tests whether a particular department of Govern¬ 
ment or the public undertaking is an industry or not have 
been now laid down In what is known as Bangalore Water 
Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A, Rajappa and others re¬ 
ported in 1978 (I) ELI, page 349. I| has been held therein:- 

“1, (a) Where (i) Systematic activity; (ii) organised by 
co-operation between employer and employee (the 
direct and substantial element Is commerelall; fliil 
for the production and/or distribution of goods and 
sertfi^ calculated to satisfy human wants and 


wishes (not spiritual or religious) but inclusive of 
maierial things of services geared to celestial bliss 

i.e. making on a large-scale of (prasad or food) 
prima facie ilierc ir an industry in that enterprise, 

(b) Absence of profit-motive or gainful objective is ir¬ 

relevant, be the venture in the public. Joint or 
private or other sector. 

(c) The true focus is functional and the decisive test 

is the nature of the activity witli special 'emphasis 
on the employer employee relations, 

(.d) If the orgainsatiou is a trade or busiriess, it does 
not cease to be one because of philanthropy uni- 
mating the undertaking.” 

In the same case it was further held that sovereign fun- 
dtions strictly imdcrstood alone qualify for ejccmption, not 
the welfare activities or economic adventures undertaken by 
Oovemment or statutory bodies. It was further held, eveft 
in departments discharging sovereign functions, if there arc 
imits which are industries and they are substantially severable 
then they can be considered to come within S. 2(1). While 
laying down dominant nature test the Lordships of Supreme 
Court furtlier observed that where complex activities arc 
carried on, some of which may qualify for exemption, others 
not, involves employees of tlio total undertaking, the pre¬ 
dominant natirre of the services and the integrated nature 
of the departments as explaiivcd in the Nagpur Corporation 
case will be the true test. Keeping these various tost.s in 
view, if we turn to thp business transacted by the depart¬ 
ment namely the Civil Aviation Department we find that 
the said department deals with various activities which 
are enumerated at page 51 of the book called Allocation of 
Business Rules, 1961 published by the Government of India. 
Now this department appears to be an amalgam of 
Ministry of Civil Aviation and Tourism, yet since we arc 
concerned with the Civil Aviation Department, reference 
to the activities of Ministry of Tourism which are enume¬ 
rated there would be irrelevant and therefore redundant. 
The activities of Civil Aviation Department as stated are as 
follows:— 


1. Metoorogical Organisation, 

2. Aircraft and air navigation; provision of Aerodromes 

regulation and organisation of air traffic and of 
aerodromes excepting Sanitary control of air navi¬ 
gation. 

3. Beacons and other provision for the safety of air¬ 

craft. 

4. Carriage of passengers and goods by air. 

4A. International Civil Aviation Organishtion (ICAO). 

4B. International Air Transport Association (IaTA). 

4C. Commonwealth Air Transport (CATC); 

4D. Commonwealth Advisory Acronatutical Research 
Council, 

5. Corporation cstablislied under the Air Corporation 

Act. 1953. 

6. Chief Commissioner of Railways Safely. 

7. Now it is true that the list especially at S. No, 4 
refers to carriage of passengers and goods by air. Yet the 
Aircraft Act, 1934 and the Aircraft Rules, 1937 and from 
the preamble as well as from the various inles it is evident 
that the operation of Aircraft whether for the purpose of 
carrying goods or passenger has been left to the under¬ 
takings which may be recognised for the said purpose and 
what is left with the dcpait.ment is the function- of super¬ 
vision and control, which is an administrative activity and 
is different from the activity of carriage of passengers and 
goods. It is pertinent to note that the Civil A'Watlon Depart¬ 
ment was never dcclaicd to be a public utility service as is 
defined under Section 2(n) of the Industrial Disputes Act. 
Therefore what impressed the Lordship of Kerala High 
Court In Bhaskaran Vs. Sub-DIvlslonal Officer, 1982 (II), 
LIT, page 248 while, declaring the Posts and Telegraph De¬ 
partment to be an industry is conspicuously absent in the

y. ;>o tar as sovereign or regal functions of the State 
arc coriLeiued there wni be liiire dillieuliy to nold it to ec 
not an mtlusiry. similarly the second category namely eco- 
noinic advenmres wnicn clearly and unmistakably partako 
of tne uaitirc of irauntg or busmct-a activity and there will 
be ludo uihiculty to iioid that it was eviueniiy a business 
activity and therefore an industiy. in the case of third 
category namely ihe organized activity not stamped with 
tne tuiul indicia -ol business yet bearing a resemblance to or 
bemg analogous to trade and industry it was observed that 
this part or category would bo covered by Bangalore Watei 
Supply and as such can be icrnicd as an industry. 

10. Then remains the fourth category namely the lesi 
duary organ.sed (joveimnental activities which may not 
come within the ambit of the aloresuid three categories. In 
this regard it was observed that it would be a governmental 
function outside the ambit of the term industry as defined 
in Section 2(1) of the Act. The true test therefore regaid- 
ing a particular department of Cioveinment even if it is 
not sovereign or the regal activity, is whether the same 
falls in the second or third category and if we can note 
a finding in the affirmative then alone a particular depart¬ 
ment though of Ccnlral oi State Government can still be 
declared to be an industry but otherwise not. Apart from 
trade and business the Government has various govern¬ 
mental functions which as head of the slate it must under¬ 
take and merely because it was not declared an the Sovere¬ 
ign or the regal function as held in the case above referred 
to, we rannot jump to the conclusion that when it is not 
the sovereign or the regal function it must be an industry. 
As held by the Lordships of the Punjab and Haryana High 
Court there still remains the fourth category involving the 
residuary li.ganised governmental activity which does not 
fall within the ambit of any of the three categories and 
when neither strictly ‘trade or business’ in nature nor even 
remotely resembling such activities may not be sovereign 
or the regal function, .still it would not fall within the ambit 
of industry. 

11. With this test in mind if we again revert to su"h 
activities undetraken by the Ministry of Civil Aviation, we 
find nowhere that either it can be said to be trade or busi¬ 
ness. Certainly, had the carriage of passengers and goods bv 
air remained as part of the activities of the d-partrrent 
things would have been d'fferent but since under legislation 
the activity has been now entrusted to some corporation, 
merely because the Jenarlment has a general control which 
is ne'essary as a State in the interest of citizens, the activity 
of the corporation cannot be that of the department so 
as 10 convert it into an industry. T therefore hold that the 
activities as thev stand do not bring the case under Sec¬ 
tion 2(J) of the Act.

2, According to the petitioners, they were woiking as 
Class IV eniNoyces with the designation of Casual-watch¬ 
men under the Respdt. Corporation for a number of years 
but their services were not regularised despite repeated en¬ 
treaties. They therefore, raised an issue which was taken 
Up to the Conciliation machinery presided over by the 
A.L,C.(C). However the matter could not be thrashed out 
to the satisfaction of the parties and so a failure report 
was submitted by the concerned A.L.C. (C) to the Ministry 
of Labour but meanwhile the Corporation terminated the 
services of Sarvshri Bant Singh and 82 others as mentioned 
in the above noted annexure. 


cessions granted to the re-employed Ex-servicc men in the 
^ar 1976 under the directions of the Finance Ministry as 
per instructions Ex. W4 and \V5. And, perhaps, it was in 
realisation of the futility of theiildefcncc that the manage¬ 
ment neither controverted the petitioners’ claim by way 
of any definite plea in the written statement nor by lead¬ 
ing any evidence during the course of trial before this 
Tribual. 

9. As regards the other aspect of the dispute, from the 
disclosures in para No. 5 of the written statement and the 
admission of the petitioners’ authorised representative 
Shri IP. K. Singla, taken down on the Tribunal’s record on 
18-2-1986, it is abundantly clear that but for a limited num¬ 
ber of employees the rest have since been rc-engaged by 
the Respdt. Corpn, It was in view there of that Shri Sipgla 
sought relief only for the 24 workers mentioned at serial 
numbers 1 to 3, 5, 7, 9 to 18, 22, 23, 56, 58 to 61, 66, 
69 and 70 in the abov'c Annexure. 

10. At the risk of repetition it may be emphasised that 
the burden of Corporation’s defence was that all the peti¬ 
tioners, including those rc-engaged, were drawn on loan 
basis from the Punjab Home Guards to supplement the 
Watch and Ward ^aff on Open Storage Complexe.s, But 
surprisingly enough no evidence was adduced tp show whe¬ 
ther the petitioners were in permanent (or even temporary) 
employment of the Punjab Home Guards so as to confer 
the status of parent-department on the latter Organisation; 
or as to whether any written requisition was ever sent to 
the Punjab Home Guards and if so on what terms and 
conditions the Punjab Homo Ouarus had agreed to lend 
their services. 


3. The petitioners again approached the A.L.C. in a bid 
to avoid their terminations which were stated to be in vio¬ 
lation of the mandatory provisions of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act but once again no amicable settlement could be 
arrived at between the parlies and thus forced by the cir¬ 
cumstances, the Appropriate Govt, referred the dispute to 
this Tribunal, as indicated here-in-before. 

4. In the same sequence it may also be worthwhile to 
note that a number of Class IV employees working under 
the Respdt. Corporation were Ex-.sen'icemen who wanted 
to avail of certain benefits .accorded to them under a 
policy decision of the Govt, for the services rendered by 
them in the Armed Forces but the management did not 
see any logic In their demand and so that issue was also 
referred by the Appropriate Govt, to the Tribunal. 

5. Resisting the proceedings the management pleaded that 
the petitioners belonged to the Punjab Liome Guard Depart¬ 
ment and were hired by it on daily wages on the rates ap¬ 
proved by the concerned Deputy Commissioners to supple¬ 
ment the watch and ward staff on Open Storage Complexes; 
that on liquidation of the food-grain stocks lying In the 
open, such casual watchmen were rc-patriated to their 
parent department; although some of them were re-engaged 
to meet the cxegencles of the work-load. 

6. In support of their claim the petitioners examined 
their authorised representative Shri P. K. Singla whereas 
the management produced their District Manager Capt, 
Om Parkash. Of course, both the parties filed a number 
of documents also whose authenticity was not disputed from 
either side. 

7. I have carefully perused the entire available material 
and heard the parties. 


11. On behalf of the management reliance was placed on 
the admission of the petitioners’ representative Snrl P. K. 
Singla that they were the volunteers of the Punjab Home 
Guards. In the same sequence Letter Ex. M2 of the Pla¬ 
toon Commander, received at its Sangrur Depot, was also 
pressed in service. 

12. I am afraid the management has tried to read too 
much In between the lines. Taken at its best the aforesaid 
evidence would show that the petitioners were volunteers of 
the Punjab Home Guards; but, then, under the scheme of 
the Home Guards Act even full time employees of Public 
Undertakings like the FCI can be legitimately enrolled as 
volunteers of the Punjab Home Guards and the latter can 
certainly requisition them on “Call-out” duties; while on 
such emergency duly thev keep on drawing their salary 
from the Public Undertaking or Organisation where they 
jrc employed; whereas the liabllifity of the Punjab Home 
Guards is confined only to the payment of some thing by 
wav of Call-out Allowance as would bo evident from the 
contents of the aforesaid letter Exb. M2 relied upon by the 
Management itself. 

13. Actually, the disclosures of this document go a long 
way to show that despite their casual service tenure with 
the FCI and an afflliatlon with the Home Guards, the peti¬ 
tioners continued to be the FCI employees for all intents 
and purposes. To be precise even while on Home Guard 
duty they were to draw their usual wagesjsalary from the 
FCI. 

14. In the same context a reference would also be rwr- 
tinent to the office orders Ex, \V15, W16 and W17, whoso 
tenor is clearly indicative of the proposition that it was 
the FCI who was the master in so far as the control on 
work and conduct of the petitioners was concerned. 


8, In 60 far as the first part of the Tcfercnce-sohcdule Is 
conremed I find no justification in the management’s hesi¬ 
tation in acco-'ding past service benefits to the Px-ServlM- 
men in the matter of fixation of pay because from the 
circular No. 126 of 1982 dated 16-12-1982 (Ex. W2) and 
office order dated 19-4-1983 (Ex. W3) there remains no 
manner of doubt that at one stage the Respdt. Corporation 
had agreed to abide by the policy decision to gtve pay 
benefit to the Ex-service men for the services rendered by 
them in the Armed-forces. As a matter of fact l^th these 
circulars wore a sort of improvement on the previous con¬ 


15, It thus becomes apparent that the FCI was not only 
the pay master of the petitioners but also had an eflec- 
tive control on their work and conduct so as to qualify 
as the Employer. 


16. At this St, age it may further be worthwhile to note 
that at an earlier stage also a number of similar references 
were received from the Appropriate Govt, to determine 
such disputes, the relevant Awards were returned, by this 
Tribunal in favour of the workmen. Feeling issalisfied

nr H—Tjns 3(ii}] 


•iTmT «rr : KiT^’ jq, i9sb/4tt r, isor 


■ Rcspdt. Corpn, had gone in Writ and in one of the 
tes the Award was set aside by the Punjab and Haryana 
gh Court as the learned Judges were pleased to obwrve 
: there was no master-servant relationship between the 
I tics. However the correctness of their findings ii still 
biudice before the Hon’Suprcmc Court by way of a 
K.P. But meanwhile some other similar references, on 
:-ing returned bv the Tribunal on the earlier lines, were 
nin challenged by the FCI by way of 6 different Writs 
efore the Punjab & Haryana High Court i.e. C.P.W. No, 
766 to 4771 of 1985. They were decided bv -a Division 
ench on 13-1-1986; and a certified copy of the judgement 
'ns filed on the records of this Tribunal during the course 
f hearing. Significantly enough these Writs weie decided 
11 the undertaking of the management not to disengage 
iich Dclitioners without comnlving with the provisions of 
he Industrial Disputes Act 1947, meaning thereby that 
lielr status as "workmen" within the nurview of Section 
'fs) and entitlement to terminal benefits envisaged under 
ihc Act Ibid was conceded. 

17. AdmltteJlv in the case in hand it is the common 
ground that none of the petitioners was given anv ter¬ 
minal benefits at the time of disengagement and if goe* 
without savinc that bv then all of them had put in more 
ib'>n an venr’s “continuous service'' as defined bv Section 
75-R of the T.D. Act. One therefore can not lesist the in- 
frence that ibeir terminations were Violative of the manda¬ 
tory provisions of the Section 25-F of the Act ibid. 

18. Therefore to conclude with my aforesaid discussion, 
on quashing the petitioners’ terminations I direct their imme¬ 
diate re-instatement on their original posts. However In 
view of the peciilier .situation and intricate circumstances 
the management would not bear any monetary fiability for 
file intervening period. 

19. Award returned accordingly.

and this document is signed by District Manager Food 
Corporation of India. TIt's document was piepared on

lust above his thumb impression, there is a certificate in 
the following words; 

1 hereby dccl.arc and I have rend and understood the

by the said regulntion. 

This docurrsent is a document nrepared in rejiular course

1. The Central Government, Ministry of Labour, in exer-

“Whether the action of the Northern Railway Adminis¬

Singh, Lic:nacd Porter Supervisor at Railway Sta¬ 
tion, Ludhiana with effect from 1-9-82 is justified

is entitled to ?". 

2. The petitioner was working as a Licenced Porter under 
the overall control .and supervision of the Divisional Superin¬ 
tendent Northern Railway Ferozepur and was placed at se’ial 
number 1 in the seniority I'st on 10-19-1981 when the post 
of Licenced Porter .Stmervisor fell vacant on the death of

The said post wij.s to be filled un on selection by sen'orpy- 
cun-< merit from amongst the l icenced Porters, Consequently, 
a written tost w.as held on 12-6-1982 which the petirioner 
also qualified. In .'til, 4 persons had pa-ssed that evamina- 
'rion and then presenl-d before the Sefection comm't'ee for 
viva-voce. As a resuft one Dhani Ram vv,a.s .selected alid 
given the relevant posting. The petitioner’s grouse was

the written test and also in the interview I'Ut iv'os wronefuflv 
denied the posting. He, therefore, rai.sed a demand which 
was turned dov'n bv the management; the is.sue was then 
taken to the Conciliation rnficbinei-y for settlement but as 
nothing concrete could emerge out of it: hence the re¬ 
ference. 

3. In support of his case the petitioner examined himself 
nnd filed a number of documents whereas the raanas'ement 
felt contended with the filinv of conies of the in'e-view 
call to ::)) the f-'.nndid.ate.s mcruding the petitioner and the 
pojtin.'j orders of Dhani Rum. 

4. I have carefully pertssed the entire available materinl 
and heard the parties. 

5. The documents tuoduced by the management lead us 
no where because of tbe ccnimon ground that the petitioner 
was put tin before ihc Selection Committee and ignored 
in f.ivoui of Dliani Ram. However, the crucial point is as 
to whether the ma.ra.acment was justified in reietfiny him and 
selectina: Dhani Ram for fhe post of Licenced Porter Super¬ 
visor dvsnife his undisru'ed service seniori'vt Of course 
when the scFcction is held on nicrit-cum-'cnioritv basis and 
file candidates ate also made to go through (be dr-ll of a

faction of the Selection Comcniftee to pick up the best card

suitable for the job. But alf the same when the aelectloni are

neither produced nor accounted for the merit liat of the

S. C. 132. 


6. On behalf of the Management it was argued that

30 more persons given In annexiirc A have put in. more than

On 23-4-85 parties repre.sentativc accepted that they have 
to give no evidence orally except documents iilcd by the

given to them by the management in some other cases the 
management admits and is exhibited as W. 2. The work¬ 
men were heard. Under I.D, Act. sub clause (ii) (a) 
“workmen has to be deemed to in continuous service under 
an employer of 12 calendar months preceding the date with 
reference to which calculation is to be made had actually 
worked under the cjnployer for not less than 240 days. 

Thus the workmen are in continuous service for fixity of 
tenure and should not be terminated in view of provision 
of section 25F ' without giving one mpnths notice and 

retrenchment compensation other words after completion of 
240 days they shall not remain a casual worker but shall have 
fixity of tenure and would he deemed in continuous seivice. 
A person in continuous service will be entitled to scale rate 
of pay and not daily wages. In these circumstances air the 
workmen of the management who have admittedly put in 
240 days will be entitled to fixity of tenure i.e. they will 
be treated on regular basis and shalj get irtonthly pay at 
the .scale rate. 

Admittedly persons mentioned in the annexure appended 
to this award had put in more than 240 days of service and 
arc thus entitled to be considered persons in regular service 
and they are entitled to scale rate of pay. 

I accordinglv give award that the action of the superintend¬ 
ing. Archaeologist, Archaeological Survey of India Northern 
Circle Agra ™ not absorbing the casual labour who have 
put in one year or more years service on regular basis on 
same scale of pay is not justified. 

The result is that the person in the list appended to this 
award will be entitled to he abslorbed in the regular em¬ 
ployment On scale rate in the management. 

r, therefore, give my award accordingly.

S.O. 1337.—In i^ursuancc of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the award of the Central Government 
Industrial Tribunal, Jabalpur as shown in the Annexure, in
