the management of General Manager, Telephones, Dak 
Bhawan, flnopal and their workmen, which was received by 
the Central Government on the 5th March, 1986. 

BEFORE SHRI V. S. YADAV, PRESIDING OFFICER, 
CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM- 
LABOUR COURT, JABALPUR (M.P.) 

Case No. CGlTlLC(R) (35) 11985 

PAR'nES : 

Employers in relation to the management of General 
Manager, Telephones, Dak Bhawan, Bhopal and 
their workman Shri Om Prakash S|o Shri Bhagwat 
Dayal, 154, Gandhi Nagar, Behind Masjit, Ujjain 
(M.P.). 

APPEARANCES : 


4. The case of the management is that the workman was 
working as causal labour on muster roll. He was appointed 
by the A.E. Auto Installation on casual basis without com¬ 
plying the departmental rules i.e. (1) without being spon .ored 
by the Employment Exchange and (2) that he was over age 
his date of birth being 10-5-1951. 

5. When these facts came to the notice his services were 
termiiiatcd in view of tire order dated 11-11-1982 of the 
M.P. Telecommunication Etepartment, 

6. He was paid the compensation Rs. 100 as per Workmen's 
Compensation Act. Casual workmen are kept only upto the 
completion of work and there is no provision to give a 
notice of one monih of wages in lieu of notice. 

7. Parties have adduced evidence. Ex. W|1 dated 15-9-1983 
is the admitted letter of termination which says.that hit ser¬ 
vices were extended for six months from 8th April but since 
no permission has been received from the higher ofllee he 
is being informed that he will be not taken on muster roll 
with effect from Sih October, 1983. This proves that 
his services were dispensed with from 8th October, 1983. 

8. Besides this, appl'iicant has examined himseif and proved 
certificates of the A.E. (Phones) Ex. W/3 dated 19-2-1983, 
Ex. W/4 dated 18-8-1981 of Junior Engineer and dated 
19.11-1982 Ex. W/5 of the Asstt. Engineer and Ex. W/6 
dated 30-9-1983 of the Junior Engineer. These certificates go 
to show that ho worked us a casual labour from the period 
mentioned therein. Applicant has also filed the duty chart 
from 27-3-1983 to 2-4-1983. 

9. Ob the other hand, management has examined Junior 
Account* Officer, Shri J. K. Pathan (M.W.l). He has stated 
that the workman, Om Prakash, worked as a casual labour 
in his department from 1-10-1979 to 1.10-1983. Thus it is 
not disputed that Ihc woikman worked continuously from 
1-10-1979 to 1-10-1983 on which dale he met with an 
accident and his services were terminated with effect from 
8-10-1983. The plea of Shri J. K. Pathan is that his services 
Were terminated because he was not sponsored by the Em¬ 
ployment Exchange .md on the date of his appointment he 
was over age. As for not being sponsored by the Employ- 
itient Exchange Certificate Ex. W/6 reproduced below throws 
light on this aspect of the matter :—■ 


For Workman—Shri Balkrishna V. Pradhan, Advocate- 
For Managepient—Shri K. K. Jain, Advocate. 

INDUSTRY : Telephones DISTRICTT : Ujjain (M.P.) 

AW.VRD 

Dated ; February 27, 1986 

This is a reference made by the Government ot India in 
the Ministry of Labour for adjudication of the following 
dispute vide Notification No, L-4()012(24)/84-D.11(B) Dated 
6th May, 1985 :— 

“Whether the action of D.ET. Ujjain in terminating 
the service of bis workman Shri Om Prakash, S/o 
Bhagwat Dayal w.e.f. 1-10-1983 is legal and justi¬ 
fied 7 If not, to what relief the workman is entitled 
and from what date 7“ 

2. Non-controversial facts of the case are that the work¬ 
man was working as casual labour on daily wages with the 
management frorn 1-10-1979 continuously till the month of 
September 1983. 

3. The case of the workman is that he was working as 
Wlreman Batriman which is perennial nature of job and still 
he was termed as casual labour. Vide letter dated I5th 
Sepfernber, 1983 he was informed that be wifi be not taken 
on muster roll with effect from 8-10-1983 and his services 
were terminated from that date. On 1-10-1984 while on duty 
ho was iniured in an accident and hospitalised. The manage¬ 
ment paid him compensation according to the Workmen's 
Compensation Act, The management has not complied with 


“Certified that Shri Om Prakash S/o Shri Bhagwat 
Dayal Sriva.stava of 29 La,xmibai Marg, Ujjain (M.P.) 
was employed as a casual Mazdoor on muster roU 
from the month of October 1979, directly, since 
the insrall'jtjon work of Ujjain Max-I was most urgent 
end the candidates directed by the Employment Ex¬ 
change were not sufficient in numbers as per re¬ 
quirement as Well as only a few of them had turned 
up for job. Under such circumstances casual labours 
were engaged directly." 

Management adduced no evidence to rebut the contents of 
the Certificate. Simply because the Assistant General Manager 
vide his letter dated 11-11-1982 (Ex. M/1) directed that 
those who are not sponsored through the Employment Ex¬ 
change should be straight away retrenched does not have 
force of law. Irregularity, it any, could have been regularised 
keeping in view the length of his service, 

10. Ne.xt ground urged Js over age. Management adduced 
no evidence to prove what was the age prescribed for casual 
labour and to prove further that the workman was over age 
on the date of his employment. In this regard Shri J. K. 
Pathan has stated that there are rules in hfa department re¬ 
lating to the employment of casual labour and the same are 
communicated to the officers appointing them. But no such 
rules are produced before me to justify the claim of the 
management. In the absence of any employment rules it 
cannot he said that both the conditions i.e. being sponsored 
by the Employment Exebange and maximum age which was 
a necessary for emnloyment. Even the order of employment 
has not been produced to show that there were the condi¬ 
tions precedent before a person could be eranloyed as casual 
labour. Thus I find that it fs not proved that hb tormina- 




1501 


II—3(ii)] 


»nT?t "fTr TRirff; 29 , lose/^k 8, 1908 


tion was for being disqualified on these accounts. In fact, 
the order of termination Ex. W/1 dated 15-9--1983 shows 
that his services v.eic terminated because the higher autho¬ 
rities did not further e.vtend his service period. This also 
belies the stand of the management and it appears that the 
management has tricvl to build up a new case which is not 
justilied by production of evidence and the rules in this 
regard, 

11. Slui J. K. Enthan in his cross-examination has admitted 
that at the time of his termination no payment Was made to 
him and there arc still casual labour in his department. This 
admission firstly goes to show that nature of work was of 
perennial nature for which th.^ casual labours arc still being 
employed. Consequently, it goes to show that this applicant 
was discriminated specially looking to the fact that the 
workman. Om Prakash Srivastava 1 W.W.I) hac stated that 
all the casual labours Acre appointed directly and not through 
the Employment Exchange. This is fortified by the Certificale 
Ex, W/6. 

12. Now 1 proceed to examine whether his retrenchiw-’nt 
is justified or not ? 

13. Section 2tool of the I.D. Act defines ‘Retrenchment’ 
as under;— 

“Retrenchment” means the termination by the employer 
of the service of a workman for any reason whatso¬ 
ever, otherwise than as a punishment inflicted by 
way of disciplinary action, but does not include^— 

(a) voluntary letiremcni of the workman; 

(h) retirement of the workman on reaching the age 
of soperannualion if the contract of employment 
between the employer and the workman concerned 
contains a stipulation in that behalf; or 

(c) termination of the service of a workman on the 
ground of continued iU-health." 

The words ‘for any reason whatsoever’ are the key words 
as has been held in the case of State Bank of India Vs. 
N. Sundramoney (AIR 1976 SC p, II11). 

14. Section 25? of the Act lays down ‘No workman em¬ 
ployed in any industry who has been in continuous service 
for not less than one year under an employer shall be re¬ 
trenched by the employer unless he has been given_ one 
month’s notioe or wages ip lieu of notice for the period ot 
notice. 

15. Section 25B of the Act defines 'Continuous Service’ 
in Sub-section fl). But if he does not have continuous service 
for a period of one year or six months he shall be deemed 
to be in continuous service under the empToycr— 

“(a) for a period of one year, if the workman, during 
a period of twelve calendar months preceding the 
date with reference'to which calculation is to be 
made, has actually worked under the employer for 
not less than— 

(D one hundred and ninety days in the case of a 
workman eniployed below ground in a mine; and 

(ii) two hundred and forty days in any other case.” 

16- Section 25T of the act provides that the provisions of 
this Chapter shall have effect r.orwithstanding anything 'in 
any other law (including Standing Oders etc,). 

17. In the instant case it Is an admitted fact that the 
workman, Om Praka-h Srivastava, had worked as casual 
labour on daily wages with the management continuously from 
1-10-1979 to September 1983. Thus he has worked as casual 
labour for more than three years continuously. Therefore 
his service will be deemerl to have been ‘continuous service’ 
and he is entitled to the protection of Sec. 2(oo) and Sec. 
25F of the Act. 


18. In the case of Mohan Lai Vs. Management of Mis, 
Bharat Electronics Ltd. (AIR 1981 SC 1253) the Supreme 
Court held as under 

“Niceties and semantics apart, termination by the 
employer of the service of a workman for any reason 
whatsoever would constitute retrenchment except in 
cases in the section itseK. The excepted or excluded 
cases are where termination is by wqy of punishment 
inflicted by way of disciplinary action, voluntary 
retirement of the workman, retirement of the work¬ 
man on reaching the age of superannuation if the 
contract of cmploymein between the employer and 
the workman concerned contains a stipulation in 
thatjichalf and termination of the service of a vrork- 
man on the ground of continued ill-health.” 

“In the instant case termination of service of the appel¬ 
lant docs not fall within any of the e.xccptions or 
to be precise excluded categories. Undoubtedly there¬ 
fore the termination would constitute retrenchment. 
It is well settled that where prerequisite for valid 
retrenchment a.s laid down in Section 25F has not 
been complied with, retrenchment bringing about 
termination of service is ah initio void." 

‘Before a workman can complain retrenchment being 
not in consonance with Section 25F. he has to show 
that he lias been in continuous service for noi less 
than one year under that employer who has re¬ 
trenched him from seiwicc. Section 25-B is the dic¬ 
tionary clait.se for the expression ‘continuous service’." 

The same view has been reiterated in Factory Mantificr, 
Central India Machinery Mfg. Co, Ltd. Gwalior and anpther 
Vs. Naresh Chandra Saxena (1985 LIC p. 941); Karnataka 
Stale Road Transport Corporation, Bangalore Vs. H. Boraiah 
(AIR 1983 SC 1320); Robert D’Souza Vs. The Executive 
Engineer. Southern Railway and another (AIR 1982 SC 
p. 854). 

19. On behalf of the management it has been contended 
that Shri Om Prakash Srivastava was a casual labour there¬ 
fore his services could be terminated without notice ami ^e 
necessary compensation without compliance of Sec. 25E of 
the Act. This contention is without any substance. 

20. In the case of Robert D’Souza Vs, The Executive En¬ 
gineer Southern Railway and another (supra) workman was a 
casual labour like the present workman W’ho had acquired 
the status of temiwrary servant (in the instant case the 
workman had put in more than three years deemed to be 
continuous service) was held to be entitled to the protec¬ 
tion of Sec. 2(oo) of the Act. 

21. For the reasons discussed .above, T nm of the opinion 
that termination of the workman with effect from 1-10-1983 
amounts to retrenchment within the meaning of Sec, 2(oo) 
of the Act and his termination being not in accordance with 
the provisions of Sec. 2(oo) and 25F of the Act Is void 
ah initio. 

22. T, therefore, answer the reference as under 

The action of the D.E.T. Ujjain in terminating the sendee 
of his workman Shri Om Prakash S/o Shri Bhagwat Dayal 
wrth effect from 1-10-1983 is illegal and unjustified and he 
is entitled to reinstatement as casual labour with full back 
wages and other ancillorly reliefs from 1-10 19R3, He is also 
entitled to Rs. 100 as costs. 

V. S. YADAV. Presiding Officer. 

Dated ■. 27-2-1986.

f. i lac Central Government, Ministry of Labour vide its 
notification No. L-4U0I2(5)j83-D,Il (B) dated 13-6-84 has 
icleired the following discutc for adjudication to this Tri¬ 
bunal ; 

Whedier the action of the Senior Supcintendent of Post

Prasad us Sub Post Master Munslhaxi (Pithoragarh) 

i.s jnslilied If not, to what relief is the workman 
concerned rntilled ? 

2. It is not disputed that the workman Shri Parmeshwari' 
Prasad Srivastava was woikmg as APM Allahabad. He was 
transferred to Pitlniragarh by: a telegram of Post Master 
General Lucknow on 16-4-SO with instruction to relieve him

wa.s a regular transfer by the post master geoeral in pablic

definition of workman and the post office comes under the. 
definition of industry so long its workers arc not hit by anv 
of the provisions of the definition class of workman. I 
consequently repelled that objection. 

12, The only point to be decided in this case whether 
the transfer of the workman was malafide as alleged or 
bonafide. Admittedly no written request was made to the 
Post Master General for transfer, Tt docs not appeal to 
reason why Sr, Suptd Po.st Offices did not take up the

14. In the absence of apccific denial either by Sri Ved

Ved Prakash and the transfer to the farthest place in Uttar

question was put to him on the point to creat doubt or

15. In the circumstances, I hold that the transfer of the 
workman Shri Parmeshwari Piasad Srivastava was not bona- 
fide but was malafide with the ulterior motive to fake work¬ 
man to task. The management should have charge sheeted 
for any of the allegation i.e. complicity of assault of post 
master Shri Ved Prakn.sh. this having not been done and 
the transfer being malafide. I hold that the order of 
the Post Master General Utinr Pr.adc.sh ordering transfer 
of Shri Parmeshwari I’ras.ad Srh'ustava to Miinshihari com- 
mimicated through 'he .Senior Superintendent Allahabad is 
not justified, The result i.s lhat the workman will be deemed 
to be working at Allahabad from the date he had hand 
over charge under said orders and will be entitled to all 
benefits till the dale of his voluntarily retirement. 

16. I. therefore, give my award accordingly.

Sukhdeo Das waa appointed us Peon-cum-Paraih on 16-5-75, 
Rajendra Das, was appointed at P«on-cum-Farash on 19-5-76 
and Jag Naxain Ctiondimry was appointed at Pcon-cum- 
Farash on .;4-2-51. liicy were appointed diieeily at Feon- 
cuin-FaiBiih. Pile conoeraed person Smt. Kanta KuiDari 
1^1 wus admittedly appointed as Farath on 27-19-73. So 
it IS clear that she was appointed in the Bank since before 
the appointment of hukhdeo Das, Rajender Das and Jag 
Harain Choudhary and it will oi'so appear that those thi'cc 
persons weio appointed in the station tn which Smt. Kanta 
Kumati Lai was working. Since the concerned perton wa* 
not aiiordod any opportunity to change her designation to 
Pcon-cum-Farash by the management, it has to be held that 
her seniony for promotion has to be reckoned from the 
elute since prior to the direct appointment of Sukltdeo Das 
Qijde as Pcon-cum-Farash on 16-8-75, As Sukltdeo, Das 
was the lirst employee to be appointed as Feon-cuin-Farash 
at the station where the concerned person was working the 
management cannot be allowed to play the game of hide 
atjd seek by suppressing Ext. W-1 as private and conlldential 
document and then also not designating the concerned per¬ 
son as Peon-cunt-Farash hy denying her opportunity to 
chose whether she wanted her designation to be changed 
from Farash to Pagin-cum-Farash. It wiU appear that when 
she learnt of the fact that me had to apply tor the change of 
her designation from Farash to Peon-CunuFarash she did 
file a petition to that effect of her own accord on 20-11-SO. 
The tuanagement cannot argue on this basis that as she 
had applied for the change of her designation on 20-11-80 
her prayer was allowed by the letter dated 14/22-5-1981. 
The letter of the concerned person only shows that she 
had no knowledge prior to the said application that she 
had to make a choice in writing for the change of her de¬ 
signation from the post of Farash to Peon-cum-Farash 
otherwise there was no reason for her not to make such 
prayer earlier, in view of the discussions mad^ above J 
hold , that the concerned person being senior to S/Shri Sukb- 
dcL) Das, Rajender Das and Jag Narain Choudhary should 
have been designated as Peon-cum-Farash as that was for 
her advantage and that her case should have been consider¬ 
ed for the post of Bill Collector. She was not given the 
post of Bill Collector only because she had not been de- 
sdgnated as Peon-cum-Farash prior to Sukhdeo Das, Raejn- 
der Das and Jag Narain Choudhary. She being the senior 
most for promotion to the post of Bil Collector she is en¬ 
titled to the said post of Bill Collector and the manage- 
mept is directed to designate her as peon-cum-Farash 
prior to the appointment of Sukhdeo Das. 

In the result, 1 hold that the action of Allahabad Bank 
in relation to their Patna Region in denying chance to the 
concerned person Smt. Kanta Kumori Lai, Peon-cum-Farash 
in Palna University branch of the Bank in not promoting 
her to the post of Bill Collector is not justified. The man¬ 
agement is directed to promote her to the post of Pebn-cum- 
Bill Collector in Pana University Branch, she being the 
seniorraost Peon-cum-Farash, from the date on v;hich any 
of her junior in the post of Peon-cum-Farash was appointed 
as Peon-ciim-Brll Colfoctor. 

This is my Award,

34. Here the management does not say so but say.s that 
agieement ext. W-2 related only to the case of Shri Shankcr 
Dhavan, hence not applicable to the whole body of the 
workmen of the mangement, I do not agree. Agreement 
Ext. W-3 is quite separate from agreement ext. W-2 and is 
admittedly more advantageous than the agreement Eit. W-J 
to its workmen. 

35. It has been argued on behalf of the federation that an 
employer granting favour to one of the general union is an 
unfair labour practice but If that favours turns out to the 
disadvantageohs to the management though these are to be 
Mamed and advantageous which is fair and more beneficial 
can be availed of by entire class of workmen. 

36. Association has argued that past benefits will not give 
any due advantage of seniority etc. for gratuity and other 
benefits us the notional increment on i)-5-78 were to be 
ignored for purposes of dcterminalion of length of service. 
Management has agreed to all other benefits under the two 
agreements ext. W-2 and W-3 namely leave medical facility 
etc. 

37. Arguing for the Congress Shri Dhavan drawn my atten¬ 
tion to the serial no. 10 of fhe 5th Schedule added to the 
industrial dispiiic act that breaks of continuing workmen 
temporary for years is unfair labour practice and also item 
no, 13 which dubs failure to the .settlement or acieement as 
unfair labour practice. The management did not comply with 
the provisions of Sastri .Award for its temporary workman 
as laid down in naras 495, 516 and 522 of tlie said award, 
however, the management has reconcile and came forvvnrd 
to regularise all lemporary empJoyee.s working over 240 
days in one spun of year or more. 

38. The management being victim of its own fully tendered 
larger benefits under lls agreement Ext. W-3 as compared to 
the agreement Ext. W-1, In view of AIR Madras 1947 rape 
427 in an industrial dispute all worlmien working in an in¬ 
dustry arc taken to be a party. A settlement of 1978 and 
1979 Ext. W-Z and W-2 are still valid and could binding and 
not cancelled the one more advantage to the workmen as 
whole unit should be prevail. 1 am supported with my con¬ 
tention by law laid down in Andhra iPradesh Diploma Engi¬ 
neers Association Versus Andhra Pradesh State Elootricity 
Board Hyderabad 1985 Lab IC page 897 wherein it was

Bank Limited Head Office Kanpur in reappointing temporary 


[Part il— Sec. 3(ii)] 


workmen and regularising them without giving them benefit 
of Uieir past services is not justified and that m view of ihc 
two agreement ext. W-1 and W-3, the benefits of past services 
as agreed in agreement Ext. W-3 being more beneficial to the 
workmen of that class should be given. 

41. J, therefore, give my award accordingly in Industrial 
Dispute No. 123/1980. 

42. As regards consolidated case ID No. 75/80 I give my 
award that the action of the management of Hindustan Com¬ 
mercial Bank Head Ollice Kanpur, in confirming Shri Ram 
Chandra Mishra from June 76 without giving him his past 
benefit of his past services i.s justified as the agreement Ext. 
W-1 or Ext. W-3 of the consolidated case had not come into 
clfect till then. 

43. The result is that the workman Shri R. C. Mishra of 
ID No. 75/80, is not entitled to any benefit. 

44. 1. therefore, give my award accordingly. 

45. Let six Copies of this award be sent to the Govemmont 
tor its publication and further order that a copy of this award 
be keot on the record of Industrial Dispute no. 75/80, 

Dt. 27-2-1986.
